<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, February 9, 2021
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, February 9, 2021, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant
to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
We continue to maintain physical distancing and will conduct Board of Supervisor meetings virtually by zoom. Board
Members, staff and members of the public may call and participate in the meeting by following these steps:
To Join Zoom Meeting now, click here:  https://zoom.us/j/99008409383?pwd=a2xjbWNObTI3dXMyNmxyNzFWaWZQZz09
 
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-669-900-6833 or 1-346-248-7799 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 990 0840 9383
Password: 119559
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov 1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by February 8, 2021, by 5:00 pm. for the February 9, 2021, Board meeting.4.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up
to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: January 26, 2021  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 January 2021  
 
c. Re-issuance of Stale Dated Checks  
 
d. Fiscal year 2020 Fund Transfers

<!-- page 2 -->
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration and approval of a Sympathy Letter to the family of Former Navajo Nation
President/Senator Albert Hale.
 
Presented By: Board of Supervisors 
 
b. Presentation of 2020 Census "Thank You Campaign" for Navajo County and presentation to
Bryan Cook for his Outstanding Leadership during the 2020 Census 
 
Presented By: Tammy Parise/Glenn Kephart
 
c. Consideration of award of Contract B20-12-015 Redistricting Consultant Services to Federal
Compliance Consulting, LLC.
 
Presented By: Jason Moore
 
4. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. COVID-19 Update  
Presented By: Janelle Linn
 
5. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution:
Amendment Number 3, Contract for Professional Services with the Tomhave Group Inc., to
"Advocate for the Little Colorado at Winslow Levee and Tribal Road H-60”
 
 
6. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
7. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: February 5, 2021 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 7:45 a.m. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. c.        
Meeting Date: 02/09/2021  
Title: Re-issuance of Stale Dated Checks
Submitted For: Kari Lopez, Treasurer  Submitted By: Bencita Bowman,
Accountant
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Re-issuance of Stale Dated Checks
Background:
Treasurer's checks were issued as refunds for overpaid property taxes or for investor payments. 
The checks were voided because they had been outstanding for a year or more.  The payees were
contacted and are requesting replacement checks.  All have completed the required Affidavit of
Duplicate Warrant form.  Upon Board approval, a new check will be issued to the individual. 
Pursuant to A.R.S.§11-632.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. a.        
Meeting Date: 02/09/2021  
Title: Sympathy Letter Albert Hale
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Board of Supervisors 
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of a Sympathy Letter to the family of Former Navajo Nation
President/Senator Albert Hale.
Background:
The passing of Albert Hale, former Navajo Nation President, Arizona State Legislator and Judge,
is a great loss to Navajo County, Arizona and the Navajo Nation. He dedicated most of his life to
the betterment of the many communities he called home during his lifetime. Above all, Albert
Hale’s commitment to the Dine’ people and all native issues, has impacted and blessed the lives of
many generations. He will be affectionately missed and remembered.
 
Recommendation: 
Recommend approval. 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 02/04/2021 01:53 PM
Final Approval Date: 02/04/2021

Agenda

<!-- page 457 -->
2020 Census: Measuring Response Rates
Response Rates
• National Self-Response Rate: 67%
• Projected Self-Response Rate: 65.6%
• Response Rate in 2010: 66.5%
First Nationwide Census Available Online 
• No down time
• No data breaches
• Average Response Time: 9 minutes

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. c.        
Meeting Date: 02/09/2021  
Title: Award of Contract for Redistricting Consultant Services
Submitted For: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney 
Submitted By: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract B20-12-015 Redistricting Consultant Services to Federal
Compliance Consulting, LLC.
Background:
Every ten years all Arizona counties are required to redistrict all of their supervisorial districts and
community college districts on the basis of the most recent census.  All the districts must be
rebalanced by law every ten years to keep the population of each district as equal as possible in
order to preserve the basic principle of "one person-one vote."
Navajo County sent out an RFP to hire a consultant to assist us with this task recently.    Hiring an
experienced consultant can help ensure the redistricting process remains independent,
depoliticized, and compliant with state and federal law.
The committee that evaluated the responses to the RFP have selected Federal Compliance
Consulting, LLC as the recommended contractor to the Board of Supervisors.  The company's
principle, Bruce Adelson, assisted the County with the redistricting process in 2010 and
successfully guided the County through the process all the way to pre-clearance with the United
Stated Department of Justice.  Mr. Adelson is familiar with the unique challenges that face
Navajo County from his prior experience, and also has historically provided similar consulting
services throughout the State of Arizona.
 
Recommendation: 
That the Board of Supervisors award the redistricting services contract to Federal Compliance
Consulting, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 21
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 60K-100K

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of February 9, 2021, between Navajo County ("County") and 
Federal Compliance Consulting, LLC (“Contractor”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professional services for required for performing 
all work for Redistric ting Consultant Service s, Contract # B20-12-015,  in accordance 
with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of County  
(“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Contractor represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.   This Contract establishe s the agreement under which the Contractor will 
serve as Navajo County’s Redistricting Consultant. Contractor shall perform all work 
comprised in the Re quest for Proposal for Redistricting Consultant Service s, 
Contract #  B20-12-015 and the Consultants proposal . The Consultant’s Proposal 
and fee proposal are attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control.   
 
2. Contractor’s Expertise.  Contractor warrants that it has the ability, authority, capacity 
and professional expertise to perform this Agreement .  Contractor shall provide an 
adequate staff o f experienced personnel, capable of and devoted to the successful 
accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Contractor shall assign 
specific individuals to key positions.  Once assigned to work under this Agreement, key 
personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Contractor acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee , partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the o ther.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  T axes or Social 
Security contributions will not be withheld from any payment by County, and Contractor 
shall be solely responsible for su ch matters.

<!-- page 472 -->
Page -2- Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
4. Subcontracts.  Contractor shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be per formed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed on 
Contractor by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 9, 
10, 12, 15 and 18.  Contractor is responsible for full performance of th is Agreement 
regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.   Contractor’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of car e and skill ordinarily exercised by mem bers of the same 
profession currently practicin g under similar circumstances.  Contractor’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care and 
the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed upon schedule for 
the performance of the S ervices hereunder.  Time limits established by a schedule 
approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by County or 
Contractor.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project proceeds.  The 
date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days that performance is 
delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or o ther causes beyond 
Contractor’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by th e parties’ 
representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  County shall pay to Contractor a not-to-exceed that set forth in Contractor’s 
response to the County’s RFP payment in full for all services rendered by Contractor 
pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 30 days of County’s receipt 
of Contractor’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail the work performed during 
the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of Proj ect cost shall not exceed the 
percentage of completion of the Project as appro ved by County.   
 
7. Defects in Work.   County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Contractor agrees to prompt ly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until such issues have been resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Contractor shall maintain in effect, at  all t imes durin g the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, representativ es, 
officers, officials and employees against such losses as set f orth below.  
Contractor shall provide County with a current Certificate of Ins urance or a 
certified copy of the insurance policy n aming County as an additional insured 
(except for Errors and Omissions / Professional Liability coverage).  
 
B. The following types and amounts of insurance are required as minimums: 
  
Worker's Compensation    Statutory

<!-- page 473 -->

Professional Liability    $1,000,000 each occurrence 
           and annual aggregate

           and annual aggregate 
Contractor’s Protective Personal Property  $1,000,000 each  occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Automobile Bodily Injury and Property Damage $1,000,000 each occurrence 
       and annual aggregate 
Valuable Papers     $100,000  
 
Standard minimum deductibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Contractor.  Contractor shall immediately inform County of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at least 
30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may be 
treated as a material breach by County.  Contractor shall notify County upon any 
termination of its regular professional liability coverage and shall obtain tail 
coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Contractor acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Contractor shall defend, indemnify 
and hold harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and employees (the 
“Indemnitees”) from and  against any and all claims, damages , losses and expenses 
(including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of appellate 
proceedings) relat ing to, arising out of or resulting from Contractor’s negligent acts, 
errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. Contractor’s duty 
to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with 
any claim, dam age, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily injury, sickness, 
disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property including loss of use 
resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent act, error, mistake or 
omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those by any person for 
whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Contractor may be liable) to the 
extent of Contractor’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of insurance coverage 
requirements set forth above shall not limit the scope of the indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Contractor shall retain, and shall re quire each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this Agreement for a period 
of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be s ubject to 
inspection and audit by County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.  A  
legible copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Contractor at the 
request and expense of County. 
 
11. Non-Assignment.  Contractor shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without C ounty’s prior written approval, nor shall Contractor delegate any duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.

<!-- page 474 -->
Page -4- Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
12. Negotiation of Disputes.   The parties shall make a good faith effort to res olve any 
claim or controversy or claim through infor mal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days t o 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes within a 
reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available le gal 
remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Contractor in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made to 
the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting from 
the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the extent that 
performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted by another 
cause for which Contractor is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Contractor refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards law s, 
ordinances, rules, regulations or orders of a public authority having jurisdiction; or 
otherwise commits a material breach of this Agreement.  When any of the foregoing 
causes exist, County shall give Contractor written notice and a ten-day opportunity 
to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County may (without prejudice 
to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this Agreement and finish the 
work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the 
cost of finishing the work (including any expenses incurred by County as a result of 
Contractor’s default), Contractor shall be entitled to payment for its performance up 
to the amount of such excess.  If the cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid 
balance, Contractor shall pay the difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written notice to  Contractor, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it is 
in County’s best interests to do so.  Contractor shall be paid for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and a 
reasonable allowance for p rofit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shall not exceed the total amount payable  
pursuant to Section 6 less any payments previously made to Contractor.  Contractor 
shall not b e entitled to profit and overhead on  material, equipment and services 
which were not provided. 
 
D. Contractor’s Responsibilities.   Upon receipt of a termination notice, Contractor 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all services (unless the notice directs otherwise), and 
(b) deliver or otherwise ma ke available to County copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and o ther

<!-- page 475 -->
Page -5- Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Contractor in performing 
this Agreement. 
 
14. Termination by Contractor.  Contractor may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to  County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Contractor or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other public 
authority having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a decl aration of national 
emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Contractor may also terminate 
this Agreement upon written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure if County fails to 
make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Contractor may also terminate 
this Agreement upon written notice if suspensions, delays or interruptions by County 
equal in the aggregat e more than 100% of the total number of days scheduled for 
completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of Arizona. 
 Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of Arizona 
(with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only after informal 
negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.   Every provision of law required by statute or  
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included herein.  
Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such provision has 
been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.   The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorpo rated herein by this 
reference.  
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Contractor on any 
claim based upon or arising out of this Agreement unless suc h action is commenced 
within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is val id for one (1) year from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties. Upon mutual agreement between the 
parties, this contract may be renewed up to one (1) additional one-year term. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed  by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s project manager is authorized to modify the Scope 
of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Contractor so long as the project is not 
changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
21. Severability.  The provisions of this Agreement are severable.  Any provision held to 
be invalid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other 
provision.

AMENDMENT NO. 3 
CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
“Advocate for the Little Colorado at Winslow Levee and Tribal Road H-60” 
 
In accordance with the provisions of the above -referenced contract, the terms and 
conditions are hereby amended as follows: 
 
1. Term and Renewal :  The abo ve contract is hereby mutually extended an 
additional six-month term, February 9, 2021 through August 31, 2021  unless 
terminated, canceled or extended as otherwise provided in the contract. 
 
2. Payment:  The contract price is hereby amended for the additional sum of not-to-
exceed twelve thousand ($12,000.00), for a total contract price of not -to-exceed 
eighty-four thousand ($84,000).  Time spent on H60 advocacy will be charged  to 
Highway User Revenue Funds. 
 
3. Scope: the scope is hereby amended to include the following: 
 
• Obtain written support of  Navajo Nation Council and Navajo 
Office of President for H-60. 
• Create H -60 congressional brief with historic timeline and 
description of this road’s unique jurisdictional challenge (once 
under Navajo control, now Hopi); and include state/federal funds 
invested so far. 
• Develop legislative funding strategy for H -60 using tribal written 
support and con gressional brief. Includes w orking with 
Congressman O’Halleran and Senator Kelly’s staff with goal of 
obtaining funding either through the FY22 appropriations bill or 
next infrastructure bill that moves through Congress. 
 
Except as expressly amended herein , the above -referenced contract is hereby ratified 
and reaffirmed.
